        <span class="pl-en">formParam</span>(<span class="pl-s">"firstName"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"John"</span>)
        <span class="pl-en">formParam</span>(<span class="pl-s">"lastName"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"Doe"</span>)
<span class="pl-k">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/users"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h4 class="heading-element">CSRF Prioritization</h4><a id="user-content-csrf-prioritization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: CSRF Prioritization" href="#csrf-prioritization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>If the page you use to get the CSRF token both contains a header token <em>and</em> a (different) CSRF form token you can instruct REST Assured which one to prioritize:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().config(RestAssuredConfig.config().csrfConfig(csrfConfig().csrfPrioritization(CsrfPrioritization.HEADER))). .. "><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">config</span>(<span class="pl-smi">RestAssuredConfig</span>.<span class="pl-en">config</span>().<span class="pl-en">csrfConfig</span>(<span class="pl-en">csrfConfig</span>().<span class="pl-en">csrfPrioritization</span>(<span class="pl-smi">CsrfPrioritization</span>.<span class="pl-c1">HEADER</span>))). .. </pre></div>
<p>Change <code>CsrfPrioritization.HEADER</code> to <code>CsrfPrioritization.FORM</code> to prioritize form tokens instead.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h4 class="heading-element">CSRF Cookie Propagation</h4><a id="user-content-csrf-cookie-propagation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: CSRF Cookie Propagation" href="#csrf-cookie-propagation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>As of version 5.5.3, REST Assured automatically forwards the cookies from the GET request that returns the CSRF token and applies them to the "actual" request. These cookies will also be applied to the <code>CookieFilter</code> automatically (if configured) and <a href="#session-filter">SessionFilter</a> (if configured). For example:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().
    csrf(&quot;/login&quot;).
    formParam(&quot;name&quot;, &quot;My New Name&quot;).
when().
    post(&quot;/users/123&quot;).
then().
    statusCode(200);"><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().
    <span class="pl-en">csrf</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/login"</span>).
    <span class="pl-en">formParam</span>(<span class="pl-s">"name"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"My New Name"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
    <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/users/123"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
    <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);</pre></div>
<p>Now the cookies returned from the GET request to login will be automatically applied to the POST to "/users/123".</p>
<p>If you have a CookieFilter defined for multiple requests, the cookies returned by GET to /login will be automatically stored in the CookieFilter and used in the second request. Use <code>cookieFilter.getCookies()</code> to see which cookies it has stored, and <code>cookieFilter.addCookie(cookie)</code> to add one yourself. A cookie you add needs a domain, for example <code>new Cookie.Builder("name", "value").setDomain("localhost").build()</code>.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="var cookieFilter = new CookieFilter()
given().
        filter(cookieFilter).
        csrf(&quot;/login&quot;).
        formParam(&quot;name&quot;, &quot;My New Name&quot;).
when().
        post(&quot;/users/123&quot;).
then().
        statusCode(200);

given().
        filter(cookieFilter).
when().
        get(&quot;/users/123&quot;).
then().
        statusCode(200);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">var</span> <span class="pl-s1">cookieFilter</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">CookieFilter</span>()
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">filter</span>(<span class="pl-s1">cookieFilter</span>).
        <span class="pl-en">csrf</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/login"</span>).
        <span class="pl-en">formParam</span>(<span class="pl-s">"name"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"My New Name"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/users/123"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);

<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">filter</span>(<span class="pl-s1">cookieFilter</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/users/123"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);</pre></div>
<p>You can disable this behavior by setting automaticallyApplyCookies to false the csrf config:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().
        config(config().csrfConfig(csrfConfig().automaticallyApplyCookies(false))).
        csrf(&quot;/login&quot;).
when().
        ..."><pre class="notranslate"><code>given().
        config(config().csrfConfig(csrfConfig().automaticallyApplyCookies(false))).
        csrf("/login").
when().
        ...
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">OAuth</h2><a id="user-content-oauth" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: OAuth" href="#oauth"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>In order to use OAuth 1 and OAuth 2 (for query parameter signing) you need to add <a href="https://github.com/fernandezpablo85/scribe-java">Scribe</a> to your classpath (if you're using version 2.1.0 or older of REST Assured then please refer to the <a href="Usage_Legacy#OAuth">legacy</a> documentation). In Maven you can simply add the following dependency:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-xml notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;dependency&gt;
            &lt;groupId&gt;com.github.scribejava&lt;/groupId&gt;
            &lt;artifactId&gt;scribejava-apis&lt;/artifactId&gt;
            &lt;version&gt;2.5.3&lt;/version&gt;
            &lt;scope&gt;test&lt;/scope&gt;
&lt;/dependency&gt;"><pre>&lt;<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
            &lt;<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;com.github.scribejava&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;
            &lt;<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;scribejava-apis&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;
            &lt;<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;2.5.3&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;
            &lt;<span class="pl-ent">scope</span>&gt;test&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">scope</span>&gt;
&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;</pre></div>
<p>If you're not using Maven <a href="https://github.com/fernandezpablo85/scribe-java/releases">download</a> a Scribe release manually and put it in your classpath.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">OAuth 1</h3><a id="user-content-oauth-1" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: OAuth 1" href="#oauth-1"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>OAuth 1 requires <a href="#oauth">Scribe</a> in the classpath. To use auth 1 authentication you can do:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().auth().oauth(..). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">auth</span>().<span class="pl-en">oauth</span>(..). ..</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">OAuth 2</h3><a id="user-content-oauth-2" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: OAuth 2" href="#oauth-2"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Since version <code>2.5.0</code> you can use OAuth 2 authentication without depending on <a href="#oauth">Scribe</a>:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().auth().oauth2(accessToken). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">auth</span>().<span class="pl-en">oauth2</span>(<span class="pl-s1">accessToken</span>). ..</pre></div>
<p>This will put the OAuth2 <code>accessToken</code> in a header. To be more explicit you can also do:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().auth().preemptive().oauth2(accessToken). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">auth</span>().<span class="pl-en">preemptive</span>().<span class="pl-en">oauth2</span>(<span class="pl-s1">accessToken</span>). ..</pre></div>
<p>There reason why <code>given().auth().oauth2(..)</code> still exists is for backward compatibility (they do the same thing). If you need to provide the OAuth2 token in a query parameter you currently need <a href="#oauth">Scribe</a> in the classpath. Then you can do like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().auth().oauth2(accessToken, OAuthSignature.QUERY_STRING). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">auth</span>().<span class="pl-en">oauth2</span>(<span class="pl-s1">accessToken</span>, <span class="pl-smi">OAuthSignature</span>.<span class="pl-c1">QUERY_STRING</span>). ..</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Custom Authentication</h2><a id="user-content-custom-authentication" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Custom Authentication" href="#custom-authentication"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Rest Assured allows you to create custom authentication providers. You do this by implementing the <code>io.restassured.spi.AuthFilter</code> interface (preferably) and apply it as a <a href="#filters">filter</a>. For example let's say that your security consists of adding together two headers together in a new header called "AUTH" (this is of course not secure). Then you can do that like this (Java 8 syntax):</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().
        filter((requestSpec, responseSpec, ctx) -&gt; {
            String header1 = requestSpec.getHeaders().getValue(&quot;header1&quot;);
            String header2 = requestSpec.getHeaders().getValue(&quot;header2&quot;);
            requestSpec.header(&quot;AUTH&quot;, header1 + header2);
            return ctx.next(requestSpec, responseSpec);
        }).
when().
        get(&quot;/customAuth&quot;).
then().
  statusCode(200);"><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">filter</span>((<span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span>, <span class="pl-s1">responseSpec</span>, <span class="pl-s1">ctx</span>) -&gt; {
            <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">header1</span> = <span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span>.<span class="pl-en">getHeaders</span>().<span class="pl-en">getValue</span>(<span class="pl-s">"header1"</span>);
            <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">header2</span> = <span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span>.<span class="pl-en">getHeaders</span>().<span class="pl-en">getValue</span>(<span class="pl-s">"header2"</span>);
            <span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span>.<span class="pl-en">header</span>(<span class="pl-s">"AUTH"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">header1</span> + <span class="pl-s1">header2</span>);
            <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">ctx</span>.<span class="pl-en">next</span>(<span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span>, <span class="pl-s1">responseSpec</span>);
        }).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/customAuth"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
  <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);</pre></div>
<p>The reason why you want to use a <code>AuthFilter</code> and not <code>Filter</code> is that <code>AuthFilters</code> are automatically removed when doing <code>given().auth().none(). ..</code>.</p>
<p>You can also implement an authentication scheme. Since REST Assured 6.3.0, implement <code>ContextAuthenticationScheme</code>. It gets an <code>AuthenticationContext</code> that can set headers, answer Basic, Digest and NTLM challenges, sign with OAuth 1 and 2 and use a client certificate:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="ContextAuthenticationScheme apiKey = context -&gt; context.header(&quot;X-Api-Key&quot;, &quot;my-key&quot;);
RestAssured.authentication = apiKey;"><pre><span class="pl-smi">ContextAuthenticationScheme</span> <span class="pl-s1">apiKey</span> = <span class="pl-s1">context</span> -&gt; <span class="pl-s1">context</span>.<span class="pl-en">header</span>(<span class="pl-s">"X-Api-Key"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"my-key"</span>);
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">authentication</span> = <span class="pl-s1">apiKey</span>;</pre></div>
<p>A scheme that implements <code>authenticate(HTTPBuilder)</code> still works in 6.x, but that method is deprecated and isn't called by REST Assured 7.0.0. See <a href="PreparingFor70#custom-authentication-schemes">Preparing for REST Assured 7.0</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Multi-part form data</h1><a id="user-content-multi-part-form-data" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Multi-part form data" href="#multi-part-form-data"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>When sending larger amount of data to the server it's common to use the multipart form data technique. Rest Assured provide methods called <code>multiPart</code> that allows you to specify a file, byte-array, input stream or text to upload. In its simplest form you can upload a file like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().
        multiPart(new File(&quot;/path/to/file&quot;)).
when().
        post(&quot;/upload&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">multiPart</span>(<span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">File</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/path/to/file"</span>)).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/upload"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>It will assume a control name called "file". In HTML the control name is the attribute name of the input tag. To clarify let's look at the following HTML form:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;form id=&quot;uploadForm&quot; action=&quot;/upload&quot; method=&quot;post&quot; enctype=&quot;multipart/form-data&quot;&gt;
        &lt;input type=&quot;file&quot; name=&quot;file&quot; size=&quot;40&quot;&gt;
        &lt;input type=submit value=&quot;Upload!&quot;&gt;
&lt;/form&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">form</span> <span class="pl-c1">id</span>="<span class="pl-s">uploadForm</span>" <span class="pl-c1">action</span>="<span class="pl-s">/upload</span>" <span class="pl-c1">method</span>="<span class="pl-s">post</span>" <span class="pl-c1">enctype</span>="<span class="pl-s">multipart/form-data</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
        <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">input</span> <span class="pl-c1">type</span>="<span class="pl-s">file</span>" <span class="pl-c1">name</span>="<span class="pl-s">file</span>" <span class="pl-c1">size</span>="<span class="pl-s">40</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
        <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">input</span> <span class="pl-c1">type</span>=<span class="pl-s">submit</span> <span class="pl-c1">value</span>="<span class="pl-s">Upload!</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">form</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>The control name in this case is the name of the input tag with name "file". If you have a different control name then you need to specify it:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().
        multiPart(&quot;controlName&quot;, new File(&quot;/path/to/file&quot;)).
when().
        post(&quot;/upload&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">multiPart</span>(<span class="pl-s">"controlName"</span>, <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">File</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/path/to/file"</span>)).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/upload"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>It's also possible to supply multiple "multi-parts" entities in the same request:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="byte[] someData = ..
given().
        multiPart(&quot;controlName1&quot;, new File(&quot;/path/to/file&quot;)).
        multiPart(&quot;controlName2&quot;, &quot;my_file_name.txt&quot;, someData).
        multiPart(&quot;controlName3&quot;, someJavaObject, &quot;application/json&quot;).
when().
        post(&quot;/upload&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">byte</span>[] <span class="pl-s1">someData</span> = ..
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">multiPart</span>(<span class="pl-s">"controlName1"</span>, <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">File</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/path/to/file"</span>)).
        <span class="pl-en">multiPart</span>(<span class="pl-s">"controlName2"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"my_file_name.txt"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">someData</span>).
        <span class="pl-en">multiPart</span>(<span class="pl-s">"controlName3"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">someJavaObject</span>, <span class="pl-s">"application/json"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/upload"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>For more advanced use cases you can make use of the <a href="http://static.javadoc.io/io.rest-assured/rest-assured/6.3.0/io/restassured/builder/MultiPartSpecBuilder.html" rel="nofollow">MultiPartSpecBuilder</a>. For example:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Greeting greeting = new Greeting();
greeting.setFirstName(&quot;John&quot;);
greeting.setLastName(&quot;Doe&quot;);

given().
        multiPart(new MultiPartSpecBuilder(greeting, ObjectMapperType.JACKSON_2)
                .fileName(&quot;greeting.json&quot;)
                .controlName(&quot;text&quot;)
                .mimeType(&quot;application/vnd.custom+json&quot;).build()).
when().
        post(&quot;/multipart/json&quot;).
then().
        statusCode(200);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Greeting</span> <span class="pl-s1">greeting</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">Greeting</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">greeting</span>.<span class="pl-en">setFirstName</span>(<span class="pl-s">"John"</span>);
<span class="pl-s1">greeting</span>.<span class="pl-en">setLastName</span>(<span class="pl-s">"Doe"</span>);

<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">multiPart</span>(<span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">MultiPartSpecBuilder</span>(<span class="pl-s1">greeting</span>, <span class="pl-smi">ObjectMapperType</span>.<span class="pl-c1">JACKSON_2</span>)
                .<span class="pl-en">fileName</span>(<span class="pl-s">"greeting.json"</span>)
                .<span class="pl-en">controlName</span>(<span class="pl-s">"text"</span>)
                .<span class="pl-en">mimeType</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/vnd.custom+json"</span>).<span class="pl-en">build</span>()).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/multipart/json"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);</pre></div>
<p>You can specify, among other things, the default <code>control name</code> and filename using the <a href="http://static.javadoc.io/io.rest-assured/rest-assured/6.3.0/io/restassured/config/MultiPartConfig.html" rel="nofollow">MultiPartConfig</a>. For example:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().config(config().multiPartConfig(multiPartConfig().defaultControlName(&quot;something-else&quot;))). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">config</span>(<span class="pl-en">config</span>().<span class="pl-en">multiPartConfig</span>(<span class="pl-en">multiPartConfig</span>().<span class="pl-en">defaultControlName</span>(<span class="pl-s">"something-else"</span>))). ..</pre></div>
<p>This will configure the default control name to be "something-else" instead of "file".</p>
<p>For additional info refer to <a href="http://blog.jayway.com/2011/09/15/multipart-form-data-file-uploading-made-simple-with-rest-assured/" rel="nofollow">this</a> blog post.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Object Mapping</h1><a id="user-content-object-mapping" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Object Mapping" href="#object-mapping"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>REST Assured supports mapping Java objects to and from JSON and XML. For JSON you need to have either Jackson, Jackson2, Jackson3, Gson, Yasson or Johnzon in the classpath and for XML you need Jakarta EE or JAXB.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Jakarta EE</h2><a id="user-content-jakarta-ee" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Jakarta EE" href="#jakarta-ee"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>To use Jakarta EE for XML object mapping you need to include the following dependencies:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-xml notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;dependency&gt;
    &lt;groupId&gt;jakarta.xml.bind&lt;/groupId&gt;
    &lt;artifactId&gt;jakarta.xml.bind-api&lt;/artifactId&gt;
    &lt;version&gt;3.0.1&lt;/version&gt;
&lt;/dependency&gt;
&lt;dependency&gt;
    &lt;groupId&gt;org.glassfish.jaxb&lt;/groupId&gt;
    &lt;artifactId&gt;jaxb-runtime&lt;/artifactId&gt;
    &lt;version&gt;3.0.2&lt;/version&gt;
&lt;/dependency&gt;"><pre>&lt;<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;jakarta.xml.bind&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;jakarta.xml.bind-api&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;3.0.1&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;
&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
&lt;<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;org.glassfish.jaxb&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;jaxb-runtime&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;3.0.2&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;
&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">JAXB</h2><a id="user-content-jaxb" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: JAXB" href="#jaxb"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>JAXB can be used to serialize/deserialize XML with REST Assured. From version 4.5.0 it is not included by default, so if you're using a newer version of Java, such as Java 17, you need to add these two dependencies or use <a href="#jakarta-ee">Jakarta EE</a>:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-xml notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;dependency&gt;
    &lt;groupId&gt;javax.xml.bind&lt;/groupId&gt;
    &lt;artifactId&gt;jaxb-api&lt;/artifactId&gt;
    &lt;version&gt;2.3.1&lt;/version&gt;
&lt;/dependency&gt;
&lt;dependency&gt;
    &lt;groupId&gt;com.sun.xml.bind&lt;/groupId&gt;
    &lt;artifactId&gt;jaxb-impl&lt;/artifactId&gt;
    &lt;version&gt;2.3.4&lt;/version&gt;
&lt;/dependency&gt;"><pre>&lt;<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;javax.xml.bind&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;jaxb-api&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;2.3.1&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;
&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
&lt;<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;com.sun.xml.bind&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">groupId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;jaxb-impl&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">artifactId</span>&gt;
    &lt;<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;2.3.4&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">version</span>&gt;
&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">dependency</span>&gt;</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Serialization</h2><a id="user-content-serialization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Serialization" href="#serialization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Let's say we have the following Java object:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="public class Message {
    private String message;

    public String getMessage() {
        return message;
    }

    public void setMessage(String message) {
        this.message = message;
    }
}"><pre><span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-k">class</span> <span class="pl-smi">Message</span> {
    <span class="pl-k">private</span> <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span>;

    <span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-en">getMessage</span>() {
        <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span>;
    }

    <span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-smi">void</span> <span class="pl-en">setMessage</span>(<span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span>) {
        <span class="pl-smi">this</span>.<span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-s1">message</span>;
    }
}</pre></div>
<p>and you want to serialize this object to JSON and send it with the request. There are several ways to do this, e.g:</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">Content-Type based Serialization</h3><a id="user-content-content-type-based-serialization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Content-Type based Serialization" href="#content-type-based-serialization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = new Message();
message.setMessage(&quot;My messagee&quot;);
given().
       contentType(&quot;application/json&quot;).
       body(message).
when().
      post(&quot;/message&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">Message</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">message</span>.<span class="pl-en">setMessage</span>(<span class="pl-s">"My messagee"</span>);
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
       <span class="pl-en">contentType</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/json"</span>).
       <span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s1">message</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
      <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>In this example REST Assured will serialize the object to JSON since the request content-type is set to "application/json". It will first try to use Jackson if found in classpath and if not Gson will be used. If you change the content-type to "application/xml" REST Assured will serialize to XML using JAXB. If no content-type is defined REST Assured will try to serialize in the following order:</p>
<ol>
<li>JSON using Jackson 3 (databind)</li>
<li>JSON using Jackson 2 (Faster Jackson (databind))</li>
<li>JSON using Jackson (databind)</li>
<li>JSON using Gson</li>
<li>JSON using Johnzon</li>
<li>JSON-B using Eclipse Yasson</li>
<li>XML using Jakarta EE</li>
<li>XML using JAXB</li>
</ol>
<p>REST Assured also respects the charset of the content-type. E.g.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = new Message();
message.setMessage(&quot;My messagee&quot;);
given().
       contentType(&quot;application/json; charset=UTF-16&quot;).
       body(message).
when().
      post(&quot;/message&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">Message</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">message</span>.<span class="pl-en">setMessage</span>(<span class="pl-s">"My messagee"</span>);
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
       <span class="pl-en">contentType</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/json; charset=UTF-16"</span>).
       <span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s1">message</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
      <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>You can also serialize the <code>Message</code> instance as a form parameter:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = new Message();
message.setMessage(&quot;My messagee&quot;);
given().
       contentType(&quot;application/json; charset=UTF-16&quot;).
       formParam(&quot;param1&quot;, message).
when().
      post(&quot;/message&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">Message</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">message</span>.<span class="pl-en">setMessage</span>(<span class="pl-s">"My messagee"</span>);
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
       <span class="pl-en">contentType</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/json; charset=UTF-16"</span>).
       <span class="pl-en">formParam</span>(<span class="pl-s">"param1"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">message</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
      <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>The message object will be serialized to JSON using Jackson (databind) (if present) or Gson (if present) with UTF-16 encoding.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">Create JSON from a HashMap</h3><a id="user-content-create-json-from-a-hashmap" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Create JSON from a HashMap" href="#create-json-from-a-hashmap"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>You can also create a JSON document by supplying a Map to REST Assured.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Map&lt;String, Object&gt;  jsonAsMap = new HashMap&lt;&gt;();
jsonAsMap.put(&quot;firstName&quot;, &quot;John&quot;);
jsonAsMap.put(&quot;lastName&quot;, &quot;Doe&quot;);

given().
        contentType(JSON).
        body(jsonAsMap).
when().
        post(&quot;/somewhere&quot;).
then().
        statusCode(200);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Map</span>&lt;<span class="pl-smi">String</span>, <span class="pl-smi">Object</span>&gt;  <span class="pl-s1">jsonAsMap</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">HashMap</span>&lt;&gt;();
<span class="pl-s1">jsonAsMap</span>.<span class="pl-en">put</span>(<span class="pl-s">"firstName"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"John"</span>);
<span class="pl-s1">jsonAsMap</span>.<span class="pl-en">put</span>(<span class="pl-s">"lastName"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"Doe"</span>);

<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">contentType</span>(<span class="pl-c1">JSON</span>).
        <span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s1">jsonAsMap</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/somewhere"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">statusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);</pre></div>
<p>This will provide a JSON payload as:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="{ &quot;firstName&quot; : &quot;John&quot;, &quot;lastName&quot; : &quot;Doe&quot; }"><pre><span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-s">"firstName"</span> : <span class="pl-s">"John"</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s">"lastName"</span> : <span class="pl-s">"Doe"</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">Using an Explicit Serializer</h3><a id="user-content-using-an-explicit-serializer" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Using an Explicit Serializer" href="#using-an-explicit-serializer"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>If you have multiple object mappers in the classpath at the same time or don't care about setting the content-type you can specify a serializer explicity. E.g.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = new Message();
message.setMessage(&quot;My messagee&quot;);
given().
       body(message, ObjectMapperType.JAXB).
when().
      post(&quot;/message&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">Message</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">message</span>.<span class="pl-en">setMessage</span>(<span class="pl-s">"My messagee"</span>);
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
       <span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s1">message</span>, <span class="pl-smi">ObjectMapperType</span>.<span class="pl-c1">JAXB</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
      <span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>In this example the Message object will be serialized to XML using JAXB.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Deserialization</h2><a id="user-content-deserialization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Deserialization" href="#deserialization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Again let's say we have the following Java object:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="public class Message {
    private String message;

    public String getMessage() {
        return message;
    }

    public void setMessage(String message) {
        this.message = message;
    }
}"><pre><span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-k">class</span> <span class="pl-smi">Message</span> {
    <span class="pl-k">private</span> <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span>;

    <span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-en">getMessage</span>() {
        <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span>;
    }

    <span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-smi">void</span> <span class="pl-en">setMessage</span>(<span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span>) {
        <span class="pl-smi">this</span>.<span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-s1">message</span>;
    }
}</pre></div>
<p>and we want the response body to be deserialized into a Message object.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">Content-Type based Deserialization</h3><a id="user-content-content-type-based-deserialization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Content-Type based Deserialization" href="#content-type-based-deserialization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Let's assume then that the server returns a JSON body like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="{&quot;message&quot;:&quot;My message&quot;}"><pre><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s">"message"</span>:<span class="pl-s">"My message"</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<p>To deserialize this to a Message object we simply to like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = get(&quot;/message&quot;).as(Message.class);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>).<span class="pl-en">as</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Message</span>.<span class="pl-k">class</span>);</pre></div>
<p>For this to work the response content-type must be "application/json" (or something that contains "json"). If the server instead returned</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-xml notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;?xml version=&quot;1.0&quot; encoding=&quot;UTF-8&quot; standalone=&quot;yes&quot;?&gt;
&lt;message&gt;
      &lt;message&gt;My message&lt;/message&gt;
&lt;/message&gt;"><pre>&lt;?<span class="pl-ent">xml</span><span class="pl-e"> version</span>=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>1.0<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span><span class="pl-e"> encoding</span>=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>UTF-8<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span><span class="pl-e"> standalone</span>=<span class="pl-s"><span class="pl-pds">"</span>yes<span class="pl-pds">"</span></span>?&gt;
&lt;<span class="pl-ent">message</span>&gt;
      &lt;<span class="pl-ent">message</span>&gt;My message&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">message</span>&gt;
&lt;/<span class="pl-ent">message</span>&gt;</pre></div>
<p>and a content-type of "application/xml" you wouldn't have to change the code at all:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = get(&quot;/message&quot;).as(Message.class);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>).<span class="pl-en">as</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Message</span>.<span class="pl-k">class</span>);</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h4 class="heading-element">Custom Content-Type Deserialization</h4><a id="user-content-custom-content-type-deserialization" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Custom Content-Type Deserialization" href="#custom-content-type-deserialization"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>If the server returns a custom content-type, let's say "application/something", and you still want to use the object mapping in REST Assured there are a couple of different ways to go about. You can either use the <a href="http://code.google.com/p/rest-assured/wiki/Usage#Using_an_Explicit_Deserializer" rel="nofollow">explicit</a> approach or register a parser for the custom content-type:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = expect().parser(&quot;application/something&quot;, Parser.XML).when().get(&quot;/message&quot;).as(Message.class);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-en">expect</span>().<span class="pl-en">parser</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/something"</span>, <span class="pl-smi">Parser</span>.<span class="pl-c1">XML</span>).<span class="pl-en">when</span>().<span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>).<span class="pl-en">as</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Message</span>.<span class="pl-k">class</span>);</pre></div>
<p>or</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = expect().defaultParser(Parser.XML).when().get(&quot;/message&quot;).as(Message.class);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-en">expect</span>().<span class="pl-en">defaultParser</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Parser</span>.<span class="pl-c1">XML</span>).<span class="pl-en">when</span>().<span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>).<span class="pl-en">as</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Message</span>.<span class="pl-k">class</span>);</pre></div>
<p>You can also register a default or custom parser <a href="#default-values">statically</a> or using <a href="#specification-re-use">specifications</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h3 class="heading-element">Using an Explicit Deserializer</h3><a id="user-content-using-an-explicit-deserializer" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Using an Explicit Deserializer" href="#using-an-explicit-deserializer"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>If you have multiple object mappers in the classpath at the same time  or don't care about the response content-type you can specify a deserializer explicitly. E.g.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Message message = get(&quot;/message&quot;).as(Message.class, ObjectMapperType.GSON);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">Message</span> <span class="pl-s1">message</span> = <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/message"</span>).<span class="pl-en">as</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Message</span>.<span class="pl-k">class</span>, <span class="pl-smi">ObjectMapperType</span>.<span class="pl-c1">GSON</span>);</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Configuration</h2><a id="user-content-configuration" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Configuration" href="#configuration"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>You can configure the pre-defined object mappers by using a <a href="http://static.javadoc.io/io.rest-assured/rest-assured/6.3.0/io/restassured/config/ObjectMapperConfig.html" rel="nofollow">ObjectMapperConfig</a> and pass it to <a href="#detailed-configuration">detailed configuration</a>. For example to change GSON to use lower case with underscores as field naming policy you can do like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.config = RestAssuredConfig.config().objectMapperConfig(objectMapperConfig().gsonObjectMapperFactory(
                new GsonObjectMapperFactory() {
                    public Gson create(Class cls, String charset) {
                        return new GsonBuilder().setFieldNamingPolicy(LOWER_CASE_WITH_UNDERSCORES).create();
                    }
                }
        ));"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">config</span> = <span class="pl-smi">RestAssuredConfig</span>.<span class="pl-en">config</span>().<span class="pl-en">objectMapperConfig</span>(<span class="pl-en">objectMapperConfig</span>().<span class="pl-en">gsonObjectMapperFactory</span>(
                <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">GsonObjectMapperFactory</span>() {
                    <span class="pl-k">public</span> <span class="pl-smi">Gson</span> <span class="pl-en">create</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Class</span> <span class="pl-s1">cls</span>, <span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">charset</span>) {
                        <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">GsonBuilder</span>().<span class="pl-en">setFieldNamingPolicy</span>(<span class="pl-c1">LOWER_CASE_WITH_UNDERSCORES</span>).<span class="pl-en">create</span>();
                    }
                }
        ));</pre></div>
<p>There are pre-defined object mapper factories for GSON, JAXB, Jakarta EE (JAXB), Jackson (1-3), Johnzon and Eclipse Yasson (JSON-B). Since REST Assured 6.3.0 the factory interfaces, such as <code>GsonObjectMapperFactory</code> above, are in the package <code>io.restassured.common.mapper.factory</code>. The ones in <code>io.restassured.path.json.mapper.factory</code> and <code>io.restassured.path.xml.mapper.factory</code> still work but are deprecated. The Jackson 1 factory is deprecated too, since Jackson 1 support is removed in REST Assured 7.0.0.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Custom</h2><a id="user-content-custom" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Custom" href="#custom"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>By default REST Assured will scan the classpath to find various object mappers. If you want to integrate an object mapper that is not supported by default or if you've rolled your own you can implement the
<a href="http://static.javadoc.io/io.rest-assured/rest-assured/6.3.0/io/restassured/mapper/ObjectMapper.html" rel="nofollow">io.restassured.mapper.ObjectMapper</a> interface. You tell REST Assured to use your object mapper either by passing it as a second parameter to the body:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().body(myJavaObject, myObjectMapper).when().post(&quot;..&quot;)"><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s1">myJavaObject</span>, <span class="pl-s1">myObjectMapper</span>).<span class="pl-en">when</span>().<span class="pl-en">post</span>(<span class="pl-s">".."</span>)</pre></div>
<p>or you can define it statically once and for all:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.config = RestAssuredConfig.config().objectMapperConfig(new ObjectMapperConfig(myObjectMapper));"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">config</span> = <span class="pl-smi">RestAssuredConfig</span>.<span class="pl-en">config</span>().<span class="pl-en">objectMapperConfig</span>(<span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">ObjectMapperConfig</span>(<span class="pl-s1">myObjectMapper</span>));</pre></div>
<p>For an example see <a href="https://github.com/rest-assured/rest-assured/blob/master/examples/rest-assured-itest-java/src/test/java/io/restassured/itest/java/CustomObjectMappingITest.java">here</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Custom parsers</h1><a id="user-content-custom-parsers" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Custom parsers" href="#custom-parsers"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>REST Assured providers predefined parsers for e.g. HTML, XML and JSON. But you can parse other kinds of content by registering a predefined parser for unsupported content-types by using:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.registerParser(&lt;content-type&gt;, &lt;parser&gt;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">registerParser</span>(&lt;<span class="pl-s1">content</span>-<span class="pl-s1">type</span>&gt;, &lt;<span class="pl-s1">parser</span>&gt;);</pre></div>
<p>E.g. to register that mime-type 'application/vnd.uoml+xml' should be parsed using the XML parser do:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.registerParser(&quot;application/vnd.uoml+xml&quot;, Parser.XML);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-en">registerParser</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/vnd.uoml+xml"</span>, <span class="pl-smi">Parser</span>.<span class="pl-c1">XML</span>);</pre></div>
<p>You can also unregister a parser using:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.unregisterParser(&quot;application/vnd.uoml+xml&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-en">unregisterParser</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/vnd.uoml+xml"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>Parsers can also be specified per "request":</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="get(..).then().using().parser(&quot;application/vnd.uoml+xml&quot;, Parser.XML). ..;"><pre><span class="pl-en">get</span>(..).<span class="pl-en">then</span>().<span class="pl-en">using</span>().<span class="pl-en">parser</span>(<span class="pl-s">"application/vnd.uoml+xml"</span>, <span class="pl-smi">Parser</span>.<span class="pl-c1">XML</span>). ..;</pre></div>
<p>and using a <a href="sSpecification-re-use">response specification</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Default parser</h1><a id="user-content-default-parser" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Default parser" href="#default-parser"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Sometimes it's useful to specify a default parser, e.g. if the response doesn't contain a content-type at all:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.defaultParser = Parser.JSON;"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">defaultParser</span> = <span class="pl-smi">Parser</span>.<span class="pl-c1">JSON</span>;</pre></div>
<p>You can also specify a default parser for a single request:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="get(&quot;/x&quot;).then().using().defaultParser(Parser.JSON). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/x"</span>).<span class="pl-en">then</span>().<span class="pl-en">using</span>().<span class="pl-en">defaultParser</span>(<span class="pl-smi">Parser</span>.<span class="pl-c1">JSON</span>). ..</pre></div>
<p>or using a <a href="#specification-re-use">response specification</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Default values</h1><a id="user-content-default-values" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Default values" href="#default-values"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>By default REST assured assumes host localhost and port 8080 when doing a request. If you want a different port you can do:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="given().port(80). .."><pre><span class="pl-en">given</span>().<span class="pl-en">port</span>(<span class="pl-c1">80</span>). ..</pre></div>
<p>or simply:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="..when().get(&quot;http://myhost.org:80/doSomething&quot;);"><pre>..<span class="pl-k">when</span>().<span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"http://myhost.org:80/doSomething"</span>);</pre></div>
<p>You can also change the default base URI, base path, port and authentication scheme for all subsequent requests:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.baseURI = &quot;http://myhost.org&quot;;
RestAssured.port = 80;
RestAssured.basePath = &quot;/resource&quot;;
RestAssured.authentication = basic(&quot;username&quot;, &quot;password&quot;);
RestAssured.rootPath = &quot;x.y.z&quot;;"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">baseURI</span> = <span class="pl-s">"http://myhost.org"</span>;
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">port</span> = <span class="pl-c1">80</span>;
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">basePath</span> = <span class="pl-s">"/resource"</span>;
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">authentication</span> = <span class="pl-en">basic</span>(<span class="pl-s">"username"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"password"</span>);
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">rootPath</span> = <span class="pl-s">"x.y.z"</span>;</pre></div>
<p>This means that a request like e.g. <code>get("/hello")</code> goes to: <a href="http://myhost.org:80/resource/hello" rel="nofollow">http://myhost.org:80/resource/hello</a> with basic authentication credentials "username" and "password". See <a href="http://code.google.com/p/rest-assured/wiki/Usage#Root_path" rel="nofollow">rootPath</a> for more info about setting the root paths. Other default values you can specify are:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.filters(..); // List of default filters
RestAssured.requestSpecification = .. // Default request specification
RestAssured.responseSpecification = .. // Default response specification
RestAssured.urlEncodingEnabled = .. // Specify if Rest Assured should URL encoding the parameters
RestAssured.defaultParser = .. // Specify a default parser for response bodies if no registered parser can handle data of the response content-type
RestAssured.registerParser(..) // Specify a parser for the given content-type
RestAssured.unregisterParser(..) // Unregister a parser for the given content-type"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-en">filters</span>(..); <span class="pl-c">// List of default filters</span>
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">requestSpecification</span> = .. <span class="pl-c">// Default request specification</span>
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">responseSpecification</span> = .. <span class="pl-c">// Default response specification</span>
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">urlEncodingEnabled</span> = .. <span class="pl-c">// Specify if Rest Assured should URL encoding the parameters</span>
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">defaultParser</span> = .. <span class="pl-c">// Specify a default parser for response bodies if no registered parser can handle data of the response content-type</span>
<span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-en">registerParser</span>(..) <span class="pl-c">// Specify a parser for the given content-type</span>
<span class="pl-s1">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-s1">unregisterParser</span>(..) <span class="pl-c">// Unregister a parser for the given content-type</span></pre></div>
<p>You can reset to the standard baseURI (localhost), basePath (empty), standard port (8080), standard root path (""), default authentication scheme (none) and url encoding enabled (true) using:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RestAssured.reset();"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RestAssured</span>.<span class="pl-en">reset</span>();</pre></div>
<p>When tests run in parallel, set these static defaults once before the tests start (for example in a <code>@BeforeAll</code> method) and don't change them while requests are being made. Put values that differ between tests in the request specification instead, for example <code>given().baseUri(..).port(..)</code> or <code>given(spec)</code>. Don't execute the same request specification from several threads at once, and make sure that filters added with <code>RestAssured.filters(..)</code> are thread safe.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Specification Re-use</h1><a id="user-content-specification-re-use" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Specification Re-use" href="#specification-re-use"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Instead of having to duplicate response expectations and/or request parameters for different tests you can re-use an entire specification. To do this you define a specification using either the <a href="http://static.javadoc.io/io.rest-assured/rest-assured/6.3.0/io/restassured/builder/RequestSpecBuilder.html" rel="nofollow">RequestSpecBuilder</a> or <a href="http://static.javadoc.io/io.rest-assured/rest-assured/6.3.0/io/restassured/builder/ResponseSpecBuilder.html" rel="nofollow">ResponseSpecBuilder</a>.</p>
<p>E.g. let's say you want to make sure that the expected status code is 200 and that the size of the JSON array "x.y" has size 2 in several tests you can define a ResponseSpecBuilder like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="ResponseSpecBuilder builder = new ResponseSpecBuilder();
builder.expectStatusCode(200);
builder.expectBody(&quot;x.y.size()&quot;, is(2));
ResponseSpecification responseSpec = builder.build();

// Now you can re-use the &quot;responseSpec&quot; in many different tests:
when().
       get(&quot;/something&quot;).
then().
       spec(responseSpec).
       body(&quot;x.y.z&quot;, equalTo(&quot;something&quot;));"><pre><span class="pl-smi">ResponseSpecBuilder</span> <span class="pl-s1">builder</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">ResponseSpecBuilder</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">builder</span>.<span class="pl-en">expectStatusCode</span>(<span class="pl-c1">200</span>);
<span class="pl-s1">builder</span>.<span class="pl-en">expectBody</span>(<span class="pl-s">"x.y.size()"</span>, <span class="pl-en">is</span>(<span class="pl-c1">2</span>));
<span class="pl-smi">ResponseSpecification</span> <span class="pl-s1">responseSpec</span> = <span class="pl-s1">builder</span>.<span class="pl-en">build</span>();

<span class="pl-c">// Now you can re-use the "responseSpec" in many different tests:</span>
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
       <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/something"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
       <span class="pl-en">spec</span>(<span class="pl-s1">responseSpec</span>).
       <span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s">"x.y.z"</span>, <span class="pl-en">equalTo</span>(<span class="pl-s">"something"</span>));</pre></div>
<p>In this example the data defined in "responseSpec" is merged with the additional body expectation and all expectations must be fulfilled in order for the test to pass.</p>
<p>You can do the same thing if you need to re-use request data in different tests. E.g.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RequestSpecBuilder builder = new RequestSpecBuilder();
builder.addParam(&quot;parameter1&quot;, &quot;parameterValue&quot;);
builder.addHeader(&quot;header1&quot;, &quot;headerValue&quot;);
RequestSpecification requestSpec = builder.build();
  
given().
        spec(requestSpec).
        param(&quot;parameter2&quot;, &quot;paramValue&quot;).
when().
        get(&quot;/something&quot;).
then().
        body(&quot;x.y.z&quot;, equalTo(&quot;something&quot;));        "><pre><span class="pl-smi">RequestSpecBuilder</span> <span class="pl-s1">builder</span> = <span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-smi">RequestSpecBuilder</span>();
<span class="pl-s1">builder</span>.<span class="pl-en">addParam</span>(<span class="pl-s">"parameter1"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"parameterValue"</span>);
<span class="pl-s1">builder</span>.<span class="pl-en">addHeader</span>(<span class="pl-s">"header1"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"headerValue"</span>);
<span class="pl-smi">RequestSpecification</span> <span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span> = <span class="pl-s1">builder</span>.<span class="pl-en">build</span>();
  
<span class="pl-en">given</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">spec</span>(<span class="pl-s1">requestSpec</span>).
        <span class="pl-en">param</span>(<span class="pl-s">"parameter2"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"paramValue"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">when</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"/something"</span>).
<span class="pl-en">then</span>().
        <span class="pl-en">body</span>(<span class="pl-s">"x.y.z"</span>, <span class="pl-en">equalTo</span>(<span class="pl-s">"something"</span>));        </pre></div>
<p>Here the request's data is merged with the data in the "requestSpec" so the request will contain two parameters ("parameter1" and "parameter2") and one header ("header1").</p>
<p>Merging only overwrites values that the merged specification sets itself. For example, <code>given().auth().oauth2("token").spec(requestSpec)</code> keeps the OAuth 2 token when <code>requestSpec</code> has no authentication. To remove the authentication with a specification, set it explicitly with <code>setAuth(new ExplicitNoAuthScheme())</code>. Before REST Assured 6.2.0 values such as the base URI, port and authentication were reset by a merge.</p>
<p>A specification created by <code>RequestSpecBuilder.build()</code> or <code>ResponseSpecBuilder.build()</code> can't send a request by itself. Use <code>given(requestSpec)</code>, <code>given().spec(requestSpec)</code> or <code>given(requestSpec, responseSpec)</code> instead.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Querying RequestSpecification</h2><a id="user-content-querying-requestspecification" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Querying RequestSpecification" href="#querying-requestspecification"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Sometimes it's useful to be able to query/extract values form a RequestSpecification. For this reason you can use the <code>io.restassured.specification.SpecificationQuerier</code>. For example:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-java notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="RequestSpecification spec = ...
QueryableRequestSpecification queryable = SpecificationQuerier.query(spec);
String headerValue = queryable.getHeaders().getValue(&quot;header&quot;);
String param = queryable.getFormParams().get(&quot;someparam&quot;);"><pre><span class="pl-smi">RequestSpecification</span> <span class="pl-s1">spec</span> = ...
<span class="pl-smi">QueryableRequestSpecification</span> <span class="pl-s1">queryable</span> = <span class="pl-smi">SpecificationQuerier</span>.<span class="pl-en">query</span>(<span class="pl-s1">spec</span>);
<span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">headerValue</span> = <span class="pl-s1">queryable</span>.<span class="pl-en">getHeaders</span>().<span class="pl-en">getValue</span>(<span class="pl-s">"header"</span>);
<span class="pl-smi">String</span> <span class="pl-s1">param</span> = <span class="pl-s1">queryable</span>.<span class="pl-en">getFormParams</span>().<span class="pl-en">get</span>(<span class="pl-s">"someparam"</span>);</pre></div>
